Each caravan chooses its own leadership: some follow experienced scouts, others decide by council, while violent groups obey whoever controls food, vehicles, or weapons. They seek fuel, news, clear routes, safe camps, and lightweight goods for trade. Informal customs protect campsites, messengers, and water sources, but no authority can enforce them. UI_KCMAIN_FACTION_INSTITUTE_DESCRIPTION::en = "The Institute is a network of clinics, improvised laboratories, and specialists sharing knowledge so medicine does not disappear. Its immediate motivation is saving lives; its long-term goal is understanding the infection, preserving reproducible treatments, and training new medical personnel. Each site manages its daily resources, while a clinical council of physicians, pharmacists, veterinarians, and technicians establishes protocols and distributes scarce samples, instruments, and drugs. In an emergency, the professional responsible for the patient commands, not whoever holds the highest political rank. The Institute values verifiable data, sterility, consent, and continuous records. Scarcity nevertheless creates conflict between treating today's patients, reserving resources for future outbreaks, and permitting experimental research that might save thousands or sacrifice people who could still be saved.",

UI_KCMAIN_FACTION_KNOXNET_DESCRIPTION::en = "KnoxNet is a decentralized network of operators, radio enthusiasts, and technicians dedicated to keeping settlements from becoming isolated. It maintains repeaters, listening schedules, horde warnings, weather reports, and links for rescue or trade. No station controls the entire network: each node remains autonomous, while veteran operators agree on frequencies, codes, and standards for verifying reports. During a crisis, authority temporarily rests with whoever has the most reliable information or the link to the affected area. KnoxNet protects batteries, electronic components, towers, and relay locations, but considers credibility its most valuable resource. It tries to remain neutral so everyone keeps listening. That neutrality is tested whenever a transmission could save lives, expose a hidden shelter, or become a weapon of propaganda.",

UI_KCMAIN_FACTION_KNOX_AUTHORITY_DESCRIPTION::en = "The Knox Authority intends to rebuild a civil government capable of surviving the disaster. Its priorities are registering survivors, protecting archives, coordinating convoys, settling disputes, and restoring basic services. A provisional council of former officials and local delegates leads it; departments manage their own specialties, while emergency decisions fall to an executive directorate. Its power depends less on weapons than on maps, permits, warehouses, and records of debts and property. It offers stability in exchange for cooperation, taxes, and obedience to common rules. The Authority contains both idealists determined to preserve civil rights and administrators convinced that, without central control, Knox will fracture into territories ruled by local strongmen.",

It believes survival depends on maintaining the tools that turn a temporary shelter into a lasting community. Its interests include fuel, spare parts, construction materials, generators, vehicles, and secure trade routes. It is administered as a federation of crews and workshops: supervisors calculate costs and needs, while a council of master tradespeople coordinates projects affecting the whole network. Authority is earned through proven skill and honored contracts, not titles held before the outbreak. They accept payment, barter, or future labor and record every debt. Their central dispute pits those who see infrastructure as a community service against those who argue that free work destroys the incentives keeping the network alive.",

It seeks to secure food, seed, water, shelter, and local defense while preserving each settlement's autonomy. Common decisions are negotiated in assemblies of delegates; spokespeople coordinate agreements but cannot impose them without the consent of affected families. Command changes with the need: farmers during planting, scouts on the roads, and defense leaders during attacks. Members exchange surpluses, labor, and shelter through promises enforced mainly by reputation. The alliance constantly debates whether closer cooperation is necessary or whether every distant authority is merely the beginning of another tyranny.",

Its stated mission is keeping routes open, evacuating populations, protecting shelters, and responding quickly to threats no community could face alone. It retains a military chain of command: commanders set objectives, officers direct sectors, and squads follow operational orders, although a council of veterans may challenge decisions that waste lives. It controls weapons, checkpoints, tactical vehicles, and disciplined personnel. That capacity makes it both a shield and a potential government in practice. Some members want to answer to restored civilian authority; others believe the emergency justifies martial law, requisitions, and permanent control. It pursues profit, influence, and access: restricted medicine, weapons, identities, secrets, and safe passage are worth more than currency. It has no public command. Separate cells, buyers, and brokers know only the portion of each deal they require, while the most reputable intermediaries coordinate agreements and settle debts. Credit, favors, and the fear of exclusion secure obedience. The Syndicate avoids open wars because commerce requires living customers, yet it readily manipulates scarcity or sells compromising information.
